\section{Results}%
\label{ch:results}

The measured differential cross-sections in the bulk and search-like
interference regions, and in the \Wboson-hadronic measurement region are presented in
\cref{sec:results:bulk}, \cref{sec:results:searches} and \cref{sec:results:wbwb},
respectively.
To better compare the data with the different predictions, each distribution
is presented twice; once the unfolded data are compared with theoretical
predictions from different generators with variations sensitive to different
interference models, while in the second case the other MC used in the analysis
are presented. The agreement of the different models with the measured
differential cross-sections is assessed by computing the \(\chi^2\),
and corresponding \(p\)-values, of the unfolded data with respect
to the theoretical predictions employing the full covariance matrix.

In the following sections various distributions will be presented for each
observable quantity. The first figure will outline the inputs to the unfolding:
reconstruction-level distribution with MC/data comparison, migration matrix,
acceptance and efficiency. Besides the unfolded results systematics breakdown,
covariance and correlation matrices are also presented.

Uncertainties on predictions include statistical uncertainty, PDF and scale variations (where available).


\subsection{\texorpdfstring{\(\chi^2\)}{Chi squared} calculation}

The level of agreement between the unfolded cross-sections and the different
predictions is evaluated by using a \(\chi^2\) test-statistic.

The \(\chi^2\) are computed using the formula:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:chi2}
    \chi^2 = V^T \times \mathrm{Cov}^{-1} \times V
\end{equation}

where Cov is the covariance matrix, described in \cref{sec:covariance-matrices}
and \(V\) represents the vector of residuals, defined as the difference between
the unfolded differential cross-section and the different predictions.

For normalised differential cross-sections the vector of differences between
data and predictions \( V_{N_b} \) is replaced with \( V_{N_b-1} \),
which is the same quantity, but obtained discarding the last one of
the \( N_b \) elements and, consequently, \( \mathrm{Cov}_{N_b-1} \)
is the \( (N_b-1) \times (N_b-1) \) sub-matrix derived from the full
covariance matrix of the normalised measurements by discarding the
corresponding row and column. The sub-matrix obtained in this way is invertible
and allows the \( \chi^2 \) to be computed. The \( \chi^2 \) value does
not depend on the choice of the element discarded for the vector \( V_{N_b-1} \)
and the corresponding sub-matrix \( \mathrm{Cov}_{N_b-1} \).
In this case, the NDF becomes \( N_b-1 \).

%\section{Interpretation of the results for Bulk and Search Like Region}%
%\label{sec:results:interpretation_bulk_searches}
%
%This section presents the interpretation of the results for the unfolded differential cross-sections in the measurement-like and search-like regions, for the simple and compound variables, presented in \cref{ch:regions}. The unfolding procedure used to obtain the unfolded cross section is detailed in \cref{ch:unfolding}, and the associated uncertainties are determined according to the methodology outlined in \cref{ch:systematics}. The unfolded data are then compared with the theoretical predictions discussed in \cref{ch:data-sim}. 
%
%The unfolded differential cross-sections are presented in \cref{sec:results:bulk} and \cref{sec:results:searches}, for the measurement-like and search like regions respectively. The top panel in the figures shows the unfolded differential cross section of data (black dots) and generated Standard Model prediction (green and red point for DR prediction, violet and blue for the DS prediction) with their respective total uncertainties, displayed as a coloured shaded area. There are two shaded areas: the dark grey shows the total uncertainty and the light grey comprises all the uncertainties except for the DR/DS systematic. The reason for excluding this systematic is due to its significant influence and dependence on the single top model (DR or DS), which is not ideal for accurate measurement purposes. The total uncertainty is the quadrature sum of experimental and theoretical systematic and statistical uncertainties. The bottom panel shows the ratio of generated SM prediction to the unfolded data. 
%
%The first variable to be discussed in this section will be \mblmin, shown in \cref{subsec:bulk:mblmin} and \cref{subsec:searched:mblmin}, as it is the most sensitive to interference.
%
%As detailed in \cref{ch:regions}, this variable is the invariant mass of a \bjet and a lepton~\cite{TOPQ-2017-05} and it can discriminates very well between resonant and non-resonant \WbWb production. It is designed to exhibit an endpoint at \qty{150}{\GeV} for a \bjet and a lepton originating from the decay of an on-shell top quark.
%
%The \mblmin variable effectively constrains one of the \ttbar quark decays, since it is expected that \mbl will always be below \mtop, whereas in non-resonant \Wb systems \mbl can take any value. Therefore, events with \( \mbl > \mtop \) indicate that at least one of the two \Wb pairs was produced non-resonant. Consequently, the suppression of the on-shell \ttbar contribution for high values of \mblmin results in an increased sensitivity to \Wb systems with a higher invariant mass, exceeding the one expected from resonantly decaying top quarks.
%
%For the bulk region, \cref{subsec:bulk:mblmin}, the Diagram Removal and Diagram Subtraction predictions are in agreement with the unfolded data in the first bins of the distributions. However, beyond the \qty{150}{\GeV} cut-off, there are discrepancies between the two predictions: the DR prediction shows an upward trend compared to the data, making it incompatible with the unfolded data. Conversely, the DS prediction remains consistent with the data within the uncertainties.
%
%In the search-like region, \cref{subsec:searched:mblmin}, the DR and DS predictions envelope the data, suggesting that the overall normalisation in this phase-space is not properly predicted. In particular, in this phase space characterised by extreme conditions, the acceptances of the DR and DS samples show significant differences due to variations in the \tW fraction present in the DR/DS Monte Carlo samples. 
%
%%%
%
%The remaining sensitive variables are shown in the rest of the figures and show a behaviour very similar to \mblmin.
%
%In the bulk region, the Diagram Removal and Diagram Subtraction predictions are in agreement with the unfolded data in the first bins of the distributions. From the middle bins to the tails the two predictions behave differently: the DR predictions has a slope with upward trend with respect to data and are not compatible with the unfolded data, on the other hand the DS predictions are, for most of the variables, compatible with data within the uncertainties. However, from the middle bins to the tails, the two predictions diverge: the DR predictions show an upward trend slope compared to the data and are not consistent with the unfolded data. Conversely, the DS predictions are generally compatible with the data within the uncertainty band.
%
%In the search-like region, both the DR and DS predictions envelope the data indicating that the overall normalisation in this phase space is not accurately predicted for all variables considered. In general, neither of the two predictions is compatible with unfolded data within the uncertainties. 
%
%For the lepton transverse momentum, the discrepancy between data and Monte Carlo is attributed the mismodelling of the top quark \pt spectrum in the \ttbar simulation. This effect has been observed in different differential cross-section measurements~\cite{TOPQ-2018-17,TOPQ-2018-15}. The mismodelling occurs at the high-\pt tail, where the MC prediction is typically overestimated, and there is an additional effect at high jet multiplicity events, where the MC prediction is typically underestimated. This mismodelling affects kinematic variables which are constructed based on the \pt of all physics objects and it depends on the number of jets in the event.
%The reweighted distributions show better agreement between DR and DS predictions and the data. Especially the DS prediction in bulk region is in almost perfect agreement with the data.
%
%It has to be notice that for the measurement-like phase space the uncertainty band doesn't show any difference if DR/DS modelling uncertainty is considered while for the search-like phase space the uncertainty band without DR/DS is smaller, reducing from \qty{50}{\%} to \qty{20}{\%}. The size and shape of the DR/DS systematics is different then the difference to the data as well, even if there is a \qty{50}{\%} systematics in some bins, this shows that the measurement is relevant even with significant systematics.
%
%To summarize, while there is reasonable agreement between models and data for the measurement phase space, especially for the Diagram Subtraction prediction, the models have some difficulty in describing the search-like phase space. In the latter phase space, the interference between the production of two on-shell tops and at least one off-shell \Wb system is more important. This difference is expected because DR and DS interference schemes are designed to describe \(tWb\) events and only approximate the interference between top pairs and single tops.

%\section{Differential Cross Sections for the \texorpdfstring{\Wboson}{W}-hadronic Measurement Region}
%\label{sec:results:wbwb}
\subsection{Differential Cross Sections for the \texorpdfstring{\Wboson}{W}-hadronic Measurement Region}
\label{sec:results:wbwb}

The following section contains results for the \Wboson-hadronic measurement region in which a hadronic \Wboson can be reconstructed. Shown are the reconstruction-level distributions with all background contributions, the migration matrices, the efficiency and acceptance distributions, the final unfolded absolute and normalised cross-sections, the error breakdown, and correlation matrices for selected observables.

In addition to the primary physics observables such as the leading lepton \pt, leading and subleading \bjet \pt, we have selected several observables that are characteristic of the \WbWb topology. The reconstruction of the hadronic \Wboson allows us to reconstruct the full event kinematics. The next challenge is to associate the two \bjets in the event with their respective top decays.

To assign \bjets with either the leptonically or hadronically decaying \Wboson, we have chosen to use the modified minimax variable, as was discussed in \cref{subsec:wbwb:recombining_had_w}. Generally, we would expect that the minimax variable should pick out a \(b+W\) combination. The mass of the \( b+\ell \) pairing will almost always be less than the \(b+W\) mass due to the missing neutrino. Nevertheless, from the detector-level control plots, we can already observe some sensitivity to interference effects in the tails of this distribution. 

Now, we can reconstruct both the leptonically and hadronically decaying tops. We will choose to represent the leptonically-decaying top as the selected \( b+\ell \) combination, primarily to avoid potential two-fold mis-reconstructions stemming from the reconstruction of the leptonic \Wboson. We can also combine the \( W_{\text{had.}} + b_{1} +b_{2} + \ell \) to form a (pseudo) \ttbar system. The momentum of this system then should represent the maximum \pt of the \(WbWb\) state. 

We have also selected several spatial quantities to assess whether there is a potential bias in the predictions of the location of the hadronic \Wboson. 

\subsubsection{Lepton \texorpdfstring{\pt}{transverse momentum}}

The absolute and relative cross-section measurement as a function of lepton \pt with the related figures are shown in \crefrange{fig:results:wbwb:ptl1:input}{fig:results:wbwb:ptl1:matrices}. All of these predictions generally overestimate the data. The discrepancy is most pronounced in the high-\pt regions. However, it is observed the \POWHEG+\Pythia[8] predictions using the diagram-subtraction scheme models the lepton \pt the best. The latest NNLO predictions implemented in the MiNNLO samples improve the agreement with data.

\subsubsection{Leading \bjet \texorpdfstring{\pt}{transverse momentum}}

The absolute and relative cross-section measurement as a function of the leading \bjet \pt with the related figures are shown in
\crefrange{fig:results:wbwb:ptb1:input}{fig:results:wbwb:ptb1:matrices}. Similar to the lepton \pt distributions, the various predictions of the \bjet \pt also overestimate the observations. Again, the DS scheme for modelling the interference models the \pt distribution better than the DR scheme. However, after reweighting the \ttbar samples, the agreement with data between the DR and DS schemes becomes comparable.

\subsubsection{Subleading \bjet \texorpdfstring{\pt}{transverse momentum}}

The absolute and relative cross-section measurement as a function of the subleading \bjet \pt with the related figures are shown in
\crefrange{fig:results:wbwb:ptb2:input}{fig:results:wbwb:ptb2:matrices}. There is the same upward trending slope for the predictions of the subleading \bjet \pt as was seen for the lepton and leading \bjet \pt observables. However, this pattern continues only until the last bin where the predictions match the data well. This agreement is improved after implementing the \ttbar reweighting procedure.

\clearpage
\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\Wboson}{W} boson \texorpdfstring{\pt}{transverse momentum}}

The absolute and relative cross-section measurement as a function of the \Wboson boson \pt with the related figures are shown in
\crefrange{fig:results:wbwb:ptW:input}{fig:results:wbwb:ptW:matrices}. The semileptonic final state allows us to fully reconstruct the kinematics of the hadronic \Wboson. As shown, the reconstructed hadronic \Wboson \pt spectrum is measured with relatively low uncertainties across its entire range. However, the predictions again overestimate the observations with a slight upward-trending slope tending towards the high-\pt tails. It should also be noted that the \MGNLOPY predictions are a particularly good match to the data  even prior to any \ttbar sample reweighting. This leads us to conclude that discrepancies found in other predictions likely come from a mismatch of the \ttbar weights between MC and data. After reweighting, all predictions show much better agreement with data.

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\Wboson}{W} boson mass}

The absolute and relative cross-section measurement as a function of the \Wboson boson mass with the related figures are shown in
\crefrange{fig:results:wbwb:mW:input}{fig:results:wbwb:mW:matrices}. The reconstructed hadronic \Wboson mass is a much less precisely measured quantity, evident from the much larger uncertainties. The nominal \PYTHIA[8] samples all show similar levels of agreement with each other and with the observations. When comparing the alternative truth samples, the predictions tend to be more diverse. For this variable, the agreement does not improve drastically after implementing the \ttbar reweighting.

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\Wboson}{W} boson \texorpdfstring{y}{rapidity}}

The absolute and relative cross-section measurement as a function of the \Wboson boson rapidity with the related figures are shown in
\crefrange{fig:results:wbwb:etaW:input}{fig:results:wbwb:etaW:matrices}. Unfolding the \(y\) distribution of the hadronic \Wboson allows us to compare the location of the produced \Wboson. The nominal predictions present a slight overestimation in the region of \(|y| < 1.5\) whereas the MiNNLO samples tend to introduce a constant upward shift in the modelling of the W rapidity.

\subsubsection{Additional leading jet \texorpdfstring{\pt}{transversemomentum}}

The absolute and relative cross-section measurement as a function of the leading jet outside of the \WbWb final state with the related figures are shown in \crefrange{fig:results:wbwb:owj1:input}{fig:results:wbwb:owj1:matrices}. The additional leading jet is defined as the hardest jet not associated with the \WbWb system. It must be a light jet that was used in the reconstruction of the hadronic \Wboson or the third hardest \bjet. This variable is known to be sensitive to various differing modelling effects. For this observable, both the nominal and alternative sets of predictions indicate a discrepancy between \(150 < \pt < \qty{250}{\GeV}\). These regions are unlikely to be populated by \bjets according to the definition of the leading outside jet.

\subsubsection{Additional subleading jet \texorpdfstring{\pt}{transversemomentum}}

The absolute and relative cross-section measurement as a function of the subleading jet outside of the \WbWb final state with the related figures are shown in \crefrange{fig:results:wbwb:owj2:input}{fig:results:wbwb:owj2:matrices}. Similar to the leading outside jet \pt, the subleading outside jet \pt shows a discrepancy near the same \( \pt \approx \qty{150}{\GeV} \) region. However, with the coarser binning it is more difficult to conclude if this continues until \qty{250}{\GeV} as it did with the leading jet \pt. However, unlike the leading jet \pt, the predictions do not model the \pt distribution well in the high-\pt tails.

\clearpage
\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\mbl}{Invariant mass of a lepton and a b-jet system}}

The absolute and relative cross-section measurement as a function of the invariant mass of the lepton and \bjet combination with the related figures are shown in \crefrange{fig:results:wbwb:mbl:input}{fig:results:wbwb:mbl:matrices}. This combination is used as a proxy for the leptonically-decaying top. The predictions demonstrate relatively good agreement with the data near the top-mass resonance. However, there is a sharp transition immediately after the top mass-resonance where the nominal DS samples begin to underestimate the data. The DR predictions do not exhibit this behaviour and better model the distribution of this mass variable. While using the MiNNLO samples improve the agreement, the same cannot be said when applying the \ttbar reweighting. As expected, this variable shows high-sensitivity to single-top interference effects.

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\ptbl}{Momentum of a lepton and a b-jet system}}

The absolute and relative cross-section measurement as a function of the transverse momentum of the lepton and \bjet system with the related figures are shown in \crefrange{fig:results:wbwb:ptbl:input}{fig:results:wbwb:ptbl:matrices}. The predictions do not generally model the distribution of the momentum well. There is a noticeable upward slope to the various predictions.\@ \ttbar reweighting decreases the slope of this trend such that some of the models now agree with data within uncertainty.

\clearpage
\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\deltaRbl}{Delta R between a lepton and a b-jet}}

The absolute and relative cross-section measurement as a function of the \(\Delta R\) of the lepton and \bjet system with the related figures are shown in
\crefrange{fig:results:wbwb:drbl:input}{fig:results:wbwb:drbl:matrices}. The \(\Delta R\) variable is sensitive to the hardness of the top quark production. A top carrying larger momentum results in decay products that are more collimated than decay products from a top quark carrying low transverse momentum. The results for the \(\Delta R(b,\ell)\) displays a noticeable transition near \(\Delta R \approx 1.5\) where the predictions transition from overestimating the data to underestimating the data. Therefore, from these distributions, one can infer that the predictions overestimate the hardness of the
top quark production for boosted top quarks and underestimate the hardness for softer top quarks.

\clearpage
\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\mbW}{Invariant mass of a b-jet and a W boson system}}

The absolute and relative cross-section measurement as a function of the invariant mass of the hadronic \Wboson and \bjet combination with the related figures are shown in \crefrange{fig:results:wbwb:mbW:input}{fig:results:wbwb:mbW:matrices}. This variable is a direct reconstruction of the mass of the hadronically decaying top. The invariant mass distribution sees good agreement near the top resonance (similar to the \mbl observable). The predictions underestimate the observations immediately after the resonance but fall within the uncertainties at larger mass values.

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\ptbW}{Transverse momentum of a b-jet and a W boson system}}

The absolute and relative cross-section measurement as a function of the \pt of the hadronic \Wboson and \bjet system with the related figures are shown in \crefrange{fig:results:wbwb:ptbW:input}{fig:results:wbwb:ptbW:matrices}. The predictions only begin to diverge from unity after approximately \qty{200}{\GeV}. The discrepancy grows with \pt. After \ttbar reweighting, the discrepancy at high-\pt decreases.

\clearpage
\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\deltaRbW}{Delta R between a lepton and a W boson}}

The absolute and relative cross-section measurement as a function of the \(\Delta R\) of the hadronic \Wboson and \bjet system with the related figures are shown in \crefrange{fig:results:wbwb:drbW:input}{fig:results:wbwb:drbW:matrices}. Similar to the \(\Delta R(b,\ell)\) observable, there is a noticeable transition near the \(\Delta R \approx 1.5\) region. The same conclusion can be drawn: the predictions are overestimating the hardness of boosted top quarks and underestimating the hardness of softer top quarks.

\clearpage
\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\mWbbl}{Invariant mass of the Wbbl system}}

The absolute and relative cross-section measurement as a function of the invariant mass of the \( W+b_{1}+b_{2}+\ell \) system with the related figures are shown in \crefrange{fig:results:wbwb:mWbbl:input}{fig:results:wbwb:mWbbl:matrices}. The nominal samples all show similar levels of agreement with observations. There is a noticeable transition near the \(m \approx \qty{300}{\GeV}\) region after which the predictions begin to overestimate the data. When using the MiNNLO samples, these samples overestimate the data even more than the nominal samples. The first bin has much larger uncertainties due to low statistics, while all other bins show relatively constant uncertainties even in the high-\pt tails.\@ \ttbar reweighting improves the agreement of the MiNNLO samples.

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\mbWminimax}{Minimax mass of a b-jet and a W boson system}}

The absolute and relative cross-section measurement as a function of the modified minimax variable using the hadronic \Wboson and \bjet with the related figures are shown in \crefrange{fig:results:wbwb:minimax:input}{fig:results:wbwb:minimax:matrices}. A modified minimax variable (similar to the one highlighted in the dilepton analysis) is also unfolded. This variable again shows high sensitivity to the interference effects. This is evidenced by the fact that the DS samples generally underestimate the data while the DR samples overestimate the data.
